<h1 id="brindle-reference-version-42" data-marxy-s="0" data-marxy-e="32">Brindle Reference, version 4.2</h1>
<p data-marxy-s="34" data-marxy-e="240"><em data-marxy-s="34" data-marxy-e="240">A configuration and API reference for the Brindle job scheduler. Brindle is an invented product; every name, address and value in this document was generated for a test fixture and refers to nothing real.</em></p>

<h2 data-marxy-s="1022" data-marxy-e="1040">1. Introduction</h2>
<h3 data-marxy-s="1042" data-marxy-e="1057">1.1 Purpose</h3>
<p data-marxy-s="1059" data-marxy-e="1736">Brindle is a scheduler for work that has to happen eventually, preferably once, and never silently. It began as a collection of shell scripts on a single machine, grew into a daemon when the scripts started to disagree about what “once” meant, and acquired a configuration layer when the daemon was installed on a second machine and the two copies drifted apart. This reference describes version 4.2. It is written for the person who has to run Brindle at three in the morning, which is also the person most likely to be reading it on a small screen with a terminal open beside it, and so it prefers short sentences, exact names and examples that can be pasted without editing.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="1738" data-marxy-e="2354">Most of the system is a matter of three nouns and the promises made about them. A job is a unit of work with an identity, a payload and a deadline. A queue is an ordered place where jobs wait, and the order is a promise: within a queue, jobs of equal priority are claimed in the order they were enqueued, unless a retry has put one of them back. A worker is whatever claims a job, runs it and reports the outcome. Everything else in this document, from the lease on a claim to the shape of a webhook, exists to keep those three promises true when the network is slow, the clock is wrong, and the disk is nearly full.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="2356" data-marxy-e="3149">The design makes several choices that readers coming from other schedulers may not expect. First, Brindle prefers to run a job twice over losing it, and it says so loudly in the log whenever it does. A job that is claimed and then abandoned by a worker that vanished will be offered again after its lease expires, and the second worker will see the same identity and the same payload as the first. Handlers are therefore expected to be idempotent, and the chapter on retries explains how to make them so. Second, Brindle keeps its configuration in a single tree of named settings with typed values, and it refuses to start if a setting is unknown. A misspelt name is an error rather than a silent default, because the cost of a silent default is paid later, usually in the middle of the night.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="3151" data-marxy-e="3893">Third, and most important for anyone planning a deployment, Brindle assumes that clocks lie. It does not use wall-clock time to order events, only to schedule them, and it measures every interval with a monotonic source that cannot move backwards. When the wall clock jumps, a scheduled job is neither skipped nor run twice; the scheduler notices the jump, logs it, and reschedules according to the policy in the scheduler chapter. This matters more than it sounds. In the first year of production use of an early version, the most common cause of duplicated work was not a crash, or a network partition, or a bug in a handler. It was a virtual machine that was suspended for a few minutes and resumed with a clock that had not yet caught up.</p>
<h3 data-marxy-s="3895" data-marxy-e="3911">1.2 Audience</h3>
<p data-marxy-s="3913" data-marxy-e="4627">The settings are grouped by the part of the system they affect, and the names follow one rule: a dotted path whose first segment is the chapter’s key, as in the examples throughout this reference. A setting named scheduler.default_window.timeout belongs to the scheduler, describes a window, and sets a timeout. Durations are written with a unit suffix, such as 250ms, 30s, 5m or 1h; a bare number is read in the unit named in the setting’s description. Sizes are written in bytes unless a suffix says otherwise. Booleans are the words true and false and nothing else, since the alternatives that other tools accept (yes, on, 1) have, in Brindle’s experience, caused more confusion than they have saved keystrokes.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="4629" data-marxy-e="5357">Each chapter has the same shape. It opens with sections that describe a group of related settings, in prose first and then in a table. The table lists the option, its type, its default and what it does, and a reader who already knows the answer to the question of what the setting does can look only at the default. Sections that need one are followed by an example, in YAML for the configuration file, in JSON for the HTTP interface, and in the command line, Python or TypeScript where those are the natural way to reach the same setting. The examples have been run against a test cluster, but the values in them are illustrative; the defaults are the numbers that matter in production, and these are the numbers in the tables.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="5359" data-marxy-e="5966">Cross-references are written with a section sign and a number, such as the discussion of leases in <a href="#71-verbose-ceiling" data-marxy-s="5458" data-marxy-e="5486">§7.1</a>, and every one of them is a link. If a link takes you to a place where the question is answered only by another link, that is a bug in this document, and it would be welcome as an issue. The same is true of any example that does not run, any default that differs from the one in the program, and any sentence whose meaning depends on the reader having already read the sentence after it. A reference is a promise, too, and the promise is that nothing in it requires you to guess.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="5968" data-marxy-e="6844">Stability is described by three words. A stable setting will not change meaning, type or default inside a major version; if it must change, it is deprecated for at least two minor versions and the log says so each time the old name is used. An experimental setting may change in any release and is marked as such in its table row. An internal setting is not documented here at all, and any configuration file that names one will be rejected by the strict parser. The changelog at the end of this document lists every change to a stable setting, with the version in which it first appeared, so that an operator upgrading across several versions can read one page rather than twelve. Nothing in the changelog is retroactive: a line describes a release as it shipped, and a later correction appears as its own line in the later release instead of a quiet edit to the earlier one.</p>
<h3 data-marxy-s="6846" data-marxy-e="6865">1.3 Conventions</h3>
<p data-marxy-s="6867" data-marxy-e="7639">Operators sometimes ask why Brindle has so many settings for a system whose purpose is simple. The honest answer is that the purpose is simple and the environments are not. A scheduler that runs on one laptop needs no tuning; the same scheduler spread across three regions, with a flaky link between two of them and a compliance rule about where the payloads may rest, needs a dozen decisions made explicitly. Brindle’s position is that each of those decisions should be a named setting with a documented default, so that nothing important is decided by accident. The defaults are chosen for a cluster of three to five nodes in one region with ordinary disks and a reliable network, and they are conservative on purpose: slow and safe in preference to fast and surprising.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="7641" data-marxy-e="8367">There is a short list of settings that nearly every deployment changes, and it is worth knowing before reading further. The location of the data directory is one. The address that other nodes use to reach this one is another, because the address a node believes it has is often not the address that is reachable from outside its container. The retention window for finished jobs is a third, since the default of seven days is a guess about how long people look at results. The remaining ones concern credentials and are covered in the chapters on secrets and authentication. A configuration that sets these and nothing else is a perfectly respectable configuration, and many of the best running installations are exactly that.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="8369" data-marxy-e="9005">Upgrades follow a rule that has served the project well: the new version must be able to read the old version’s data, and the old version need not be able to read the new one’s. A rolling upgrade is therefore safe if the nodes are upgraded one at a time and the cluster is never downgraded while a mixed state exists. The migrations chapter gives the exact sequence and the checks to run between steps, and the changelog marks every release that changes a stored format with the word Changed and the name of the format. If the line is absent, nothing on disk changed, and the upgrade is a matter of replacing a binary and restarting it.</p>
<h3 data-marxy-s="9007" data-marxy-e="9033">1.4 Stability promises</h3>
<p data-marxy-s="9035" data-marxy-e="9620">The interfaces fall into three groups. The configuration file is for things that are decided once, by the person who installs the system. The HTTP interface is for things that are decided continuously, by programs: enqueueing a job, inspecting a lease, draining a node. The command line is for things that are decided by a person at a terminal in a hurry, and it is deliberately a thin wrapper over the HTTP interface, so that anything the command line can do, a program can do, and the reverse. Where the two disagree, that is a bug, and the HTTP interface is the one that is correct.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="9622" data-marxy-e="10223">A last convention concerns the examples. Hostnames in them end in .invalid, an address that is reserved so that it can never resolve, and tokens are read from the environment rather than written out; nothing in this document should be pasted into a shell without first being read. Where an example shows output, it is trimmed to the lines that matter, and a line that begins with an ellipsis stands for lines that were left out. Where an example shows a failure, the failure is real: it is the message that a recent version of the program printed for exactly that input, with the identifiers replaced.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="10225" data-marxy-e="10760">Finally, a word about what Brindle does not do. It does not run your code for you; it hands a payload to a worker and waits for an outcome. It does not store your results beyond the retention window you configure. It does not try to be a workflow engine, a message bus or a database, and every time it has been asked to become one of those the answer has been to publish a hook and let somebody else do it better. The scope is deliberately narrow, and the narrowness is the reason that the rest of this document can be so specific.<sup class="marxy-footnote-ref" id="marxy-fnref-1" data-marxy-s="10756" data-marxy-e="10760"><a href="#marxy-fn-1">1</a></sup></p>
<h2 data-marxy-s="10762" data-marxy-e="10777">2. Scheduler</h2>
<p data-marxy-s="10779" data-marxy-e="10932">Changing <code data-marxy-s="10788" data-marxy-e="10803">scheduler.min</code> at run time takes effect after the next rotation; no restart is needed. Values are read as ratios; a bare number is taken in ms.</p>
<h3 data-marxy-s="10934" data-marxy-e="10957">2.1 Bounded profile</h3>
<p data-marxy-s="10959" data-marxy-e="11068">This is independent of scheduler limits set elsewhere in the file. Related: <a href="#81-regional-policy" data-marxy-s="11035" data-marxy-e="11067">see §8.1</a>.</p>
<h4 data-marxy-s="11070" data-marxy-e="11113">2.1.1 <code data-marxy-s="11081" data-marxy-e="11113">scheduler.compact_lease.jitter</code></h4>
<p data-marxy-s="11115" data-marxy-e="11377">Changing <code data-marxy-s="11124" data-marxy-e="11156">scheduler.compact_lease.jitter</code> at run time takes effect after the next sweep; no restart is needed. If the primary is unreachable, Brindle holds the work and records the event in the audit log. Related: <a href="#1331-tlsdurable_handshakedepth" data-marxy-s="11329" data-marxy-e="11376">see §13.3.1</a>.</p>
<h4 data-marxy-s="11379" data-marxy-e="11421">2.1.2 <code data-marxy-s="11390" data-marxy-e="11421">scheduler.tiered_shard.jitter</code></h4>
<p data-marxy-s="11423" data-marxy-e="11561">Values are read as ratios; a bare number is taken in s. If a node restarts, Brindle holds the work and records the event in the audit log.</p>
<h4 id="compatibility" data-marxy-s="11563" data-marxy-e="11581">Compatibility</h4>
<p data-marxy-s="11583" data-marxy-e="11673">If a deploy is in progress, Brindle holds the work and records the event in the audit log.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="11675" data-marxy-e="11794">mode
:   The <code data-marxy-s="11688" data-marxy-e="11715">scheduler.bounded_profile</code> setting controls how a node is compacted when the disk is more than 90 % full.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="11796" data-marxy-e="11920">max
:   Operators who run 7 or more nodes should set <code data-marxy-s="11849" data-marxy-e="11876">scheduler.bounded_profile</code> explicitly rather than rely on the default.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="11922" data-marxy-e="12048">path
:   Operators who run 36 or more nodes should set <code data-marxy-s="11977" data-marxy-e="12004">scheduler.bounded_profile</code> explicitly rather than rely on the default.</p>
<h3 data-marxy-s="12050" data-marxy-e="12073">2.2 Regional cursor</h3>
<p data-marxy-s="12075" data-marxy-e="12245">If the queue is empty, Brindle falls back to the previous value and records the event in the audit log. The strict batch is evaluated once per sweep, never between ticks.</p>
<ul data-marxy-s="12247" data-marxy-e="12652">
<li data-marxy-s="12247" data-marxy-e="12383"><strong data-marxy-s="12249" data-marxy-e="12261"><code data-marxy-s="12251" data-marxy-e="12259">jitter</code></strong> — If the disk is more than 90 % full, Brindle escalates to the operator channel and records the event in the audit log.</li>
<li data-marxy-s="12384" data-marxy-e="12519"><strong data-marxy-s="12386" data-marxy-e="12397"><code data-marxy-s="12388" data-marxy-e="12395">depth</code></strong> — Operators who run 25 or more nodes should set <code data-marxy-s="12448" data-marxy-e="12475">scheduler.regional_cursor</code> explicitly rather than rely on the default.</li>
<li data-marxy-s="12520" data-marxy-e="12652"><strong data-marxy-s="12522" data-marxy-e="12531"><code data-marxy-s="12524" data-marxy-e="12529">ttl</code></strong> — Operators who run 5 or more nodes should set <code data-marxy-s="12581" data-marxy-e="12608">scheduler.regional_cursor</code> explicitly rather than rely on the default.</li>
</ul>
<h3 data-marxy-s="12654" data-marxy-e="12676">2.3 Durable budget</h3>
<p data-marxy-s="12678" data-marxy-e="12818">Raising <code data-marxy-s="12686" data-marxy-e="12712">scheduler.durable_budget</code> increases CPU use but shortens recovery time. Related: <a href="#952-metricsdeferred_channeltarget" data-marxy-s="12768" data-marxy-e="12817">see §9.5.2</a>.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="12820" data-marxy-e="12943">This is independent of scheduler limits set elsewhere in the file. Values are read as ratios; a bare number is taken in ms.</p>
<pre data-marxy-s="12945" data-marxy-e="13085"><code class="language-json" data-marxy-s="12953" data-marxy-e="13082">{
  "scheduler": {
    "tiered_profile": {
      "ttl": "5m",
      "ttl": true,
      "tags": ["default", "sticky"]
    }
  }
}
</code></pre>
<h3 data-marxy-s="13087" data-marxy-e="13109">2.4 Strict horizon</h3>
<p data-marxy-s="13111" data-marxy-e="13284">The <code data-marxy-s="13115" data-marxy-e="13141">scheduler.strict_horizon</code> setting controls how a plugin is compacted when a deploy is in progress. The adaptive channel is evaluated once per tick, never between ticks.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="13286" data-marxy-e="13364">Raising <code data-marxy-s="13294" data-marxy-e="13320">scheduler.strict_horizon</code> increases CPU use but shortens queue depth.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="13366" data-marxy-e="13493">timeout
:   Operators who run 5 or more nodes should set <code data-marxy-s="13423" data-marxy-e="13449">scheduler.strict_horizon</code> explicitly rather than rely on the default.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="13495" data-marxy-e="13570">ttl
:   The default lease is evaluated once per claim, never between ticks.</p>
<pre data-marxy-s="13572" data-marxy-e="13889"><code class="language-typescript" data-marxy-s="13586" data-marxy-e="13886">import { Brindle } from '@example/brindle';

const brindle = new Brindle({ url: 'https://api.example.invalid', token: process.env.BRINDLE_TOKEN });
const batch = await brindle.scheduler.get('ephemeral-window');
if (budget.depth &gt; 80) {
  await brindle.scheduler.update(window.id, { depth: '1h' });
}
</code></pre>

<h4 data-marxy-s="14459" data-marxy-e="14501">2.5.1 <code data-marxy-s="14470" data-marxy-e="14501">scheduler.sticky_policy.limit</code></h4>
<p data-marxy-s="14503" data-marxy-e="14721">Raising <code data-marxy-s="14511" data-marxy-e="14542">scheduler.sticky_policy.limit</code> increases file descriptor use but shortens restart time. The tiered cadence is evaluated once per tick, never between ticks. Related: <a href="#1472-authnshared_budgetttl" data-marxy-s="14677" data-marxy-e="14720">see §14.7.2</a>.</p>

<td data-marxy-s="14950" data-marxy-e="14975"><code data-marxy-s="14952" data-marxy-e="14974">priority_channel.min</code></td>
<td align="center" data-marxy-s="14975" data-marxy-e="14985">integer</td>
<td align="right" data-marxy-s="14985" data-marxy-e="14994"><code data-marxy-s="14987" data-marxy-e="14993">1024</code></td>
<td data-marxy-s="14994" data-marxy-e="15101">Changing <code data-marxy-s="15005" data-marxy-e="15027">priority_channel.min</code> at run time takes effect after the next rotation; no restart is needed.</td>
</tr>
<tr data-marxy-s="15102" data-marxy-e="15218">
<td data-marxy-s="15102" data-marxy-e="15130"><code data-marxy-s="15104" data-marxy-e="15129">bounded_policy.interval</code></td>
<td align="center" data-marxy-s="15130" data-marxy-e="15138">ratio</td>
<td align="right" data-marxy-s="15138" data-marxy-e="15147"><code data-marxy-s="15140" data-marxy-e="15146">true</code></td>
<td data-marxy-s="15147" data-marxy-e="15218">The durable quorum is evaluated once per tick, never between ticks.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<h3 data-marxy-s="15220" data-marxy-e="15244">2.6 Sticky threshold</h3>
<p data-marxy-s="15246" data-marxy-e="15429">Operators who run 31 or more nodes should set <code data-marxy-s="15292" data-marxy-e="15320">scheduler.sticky_threshold</code> explicitly rather than rely on the default. Values are read as byte counts; a bare number is taken in h.<sup class="marxy-footnote-ref" id="marxy-fnref-2" data-marxy-s="15425" data-marxy-e="15429"><a href="#marxy-fn-2">2</a></sup></p>
<h3 data-marxy-s="15431" data-marxy-e="15451">2.7 Tiered batch</h3>
<p data-marxy-s="15453" data-marxy-e="15521">The default window is evaluated once per claim, never between ticks.</p>
<pre data-marxy-s="15523" data-marxy-e="15675"><code class="language-json" data-marxy-s="15531" data-marxy-e="15672">{
  "scheduler": {
    "ephemeral_shard": {
      "interval": true,
      "path": "auto",
      "tags": ["adaptive", "regional"]
    }
  }
}
</code></pre>
<h3 data-marxy-s="15677" data-marxy-e="15697">2.8 Sticky batch</h3>
<p data-marxy-s="15699" data-marxy-e="15804">Changing <code data-marxy-s="15708" data-marxy-e="15732">scheduler.sticky_batch</code> at run time takes effect after the next rotation; no restart is needed.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="15806" data-marxy-e="15966">Raising <code data-marxy-s="15814" data-marxy-e="15838">scheduler.sticky_batch</code> increases file descriptor use but shortens queue depth. The relaxed shard is evaluated once per heartbeat, never between ticks.</p>
<h4 data-marxy-s="15968" data-marxy-e="16015">2.8.1 <code data-marxy-s="15979" data-marxy-e="16015">scheduler.regional_manifest.jitter</code></h4>
<p data-marxy-s="16017" data-marxy-e="16180">The regional ceiling is evaluated once per tick, never between ticks. If the cluster is partitioned, Brindle holds the work and records the event in the audit log.</p>
<table data-marxy-s="16182" data-marxy-e="16522">
<thead>
<tr data-marxy-s="16182" data-marxy-e="16223">

<th align="right" data-marxy-s="16198" data-marxy-e="16208">Default</th>
<th data-marxy-s="16208" data-marxy-e="16223">Description</th>
</tr>

<tbody>
<tr data-marxy-s="16253" data-marxy-e="16396">
<td data-marxy-s="16253" data-marxy-e="16280"><code data-marxy-s="16255" data-marxy-e="16279">priority_cursor.factor</code></td>
<td align="center" data-marxy-s="16280" data-marxy-e="16290">boolean</td>
<td align="right" data-marxy-s="16290" data-marxy-e="16299"><code data-marxy-s="16292" data-marxy-e="16298">0.25</code></td>
<td data-marxy-s="16299" data-marxy-e="16396">If a tenant exceeds its quota, Brindle holds the work and records the event in the audit log.</td>
</tr>
<tr data-marxy-s="16397" data-marxy-e="16522">
<td data-marxy-s="16397" data-marxy-e="16421"><code data-marxy-s="16399" data-marxy-e="16420">durable_cadence.min</code></td>

<td align="right" data-marxy-s="16430" data-marxy-e="16441"><code data-marxy-s="16432" data-marxy-e="16440">"auto"</code></td>
<td data-marxy-s="16441" data-marxy-e="16522">Raising <code data-marxy-s="16451" data-marxy-e="16472">durable_cadence.min</code> increases memory use but shortens restart time.</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<pre data-marxy-s="16524" data-marxy-e="16758"><code class="language-yaml" data-marxy-s="16532" data-marxy-e="16755"># If a node restarts, Brindle holds the work and records the event in the audit log.
scheduler:
  verbose_digest:
    interval: 8
    enabled: 64
  priority_channel:
    ttl: "/var/lib/brindle"
    tags: [default, verbose]
</code></pre>
<h2 data-marxy-s="16760" data-marxy-e="16772">3. Queues</h2>
<p data-marxy-s="16774" data-marxy-e="16987">If the cluster is partitioned, Brindle escalates to the operator channel and records the event in the audit log. Operators who run 8 or more nodes should set <code data-marxy-s="16932" data-marxy-e="16943">queue.max</code> explicitly rather than rely on the default.</p>
<h3 data-marxy-s="16989" data-marxy-e="17012">3.1 Relaxed cadence</h3>
<p data-marxy-s="17014" data-marxy-e="17110">If the clock moves backwards, Brindle refuses new claims and records the event in the audit log.</p>
<pre data-marxy-s="17112" data-marxy-e="17353"><code class="language-yaml" data-marxy-s="17120" data-marxy-e="17350"># The queue.relaxed_cadence setting controls how a plugin is replayed when the primary is unreachable.
queue:
  strict_budget:
    enabled: "strict"
    enabled: "auto"
  tiered_cadence:
    ttl: false
    tags: [bounded, strict]
</code></pre>
<h4 data-marxy-s="17355" data-marxy-e="17395">3.1.1 <code data-marxy-s="17366" data-marxy-e="17395">queue.verbose_horizon.grace</code></h4>
<p data-marxy-s="17397" data-marxy-e="17482">Raising <code data-marxy-s="17405" data-marxy-e="17434">queue.verbose_horizon.grace</code> increases memory use but shortens restart time.</p>
<pre data-marxy-s="17484" data-marxy-e="17792"><code class="language-typescript" data-marxy-s="17498" data-marxy-e="17789">import { Brindle } from '@example/brindle';

const brindle = new Brindle({ url: 'https://api.example.invalid', token: process.env.BRINDLE_TOKEN });
const budget = await brindle.queue.get('compact-cadence');
if (quorum.level &gt; 16) {
  await brindle.queue.update(batch.id, { grace: '1h' });
}
</code></pre>
<h4 data-marxy-s="17794" data-marxy-e="17836">3.1.2 <code data-marxy-s="17805" data-marxy-e="17836">queue.ephemeral_ledger.target</code></h4>
<p data-marxy-s="17838" data-marxy-e="18035">Operators who run 11 or more nodes should set <code data-marxy-s="17884" data-marxy-e="17915">queue.ephemeral_ledger.target</code> explicitly rather than rely on the default. The strict policy is evaluated once per heartbeat, never between ticks.<sup class="marxy-footnote-ref" id="marxy-fnref-3" data-marxy-s="18031" data-marxy-e="18035"><a href="#marxy-fn-3">3</a></sup></p>
<h4 data-marxy-s="18037" data-marxy-e="18075">3.1.3 <code data-marxy-s="18048" data-marxy-e="18075">queue.shared_ledger.depth</code></h4>
<p data-marxy-s="18077" data-marxy-e="18280">Raising <code data-marxy-s="18085" data-marxy-e="18112">queue.shared_ledger.depth</code> increases connection use but shortens recovery time. If the clock moves backwards, Brindle defers the decision to the next tick and records the event in the audit log.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="18282" data-marxy-e="18378">interval
:   Raising <code data-marxy-s="18303" data-marxy-e="18330">queue.shared_ledger.depth</code> increases memory use but shortens restart time.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="18380" data-marxy-e="18497">max
:   If a deploy is in progress, Brindle escalates to the operator channel and records the event in the audit log.</p>
<h3 data-marxy-s="18499" data-marxy-e="18524">3.2 Compact handshake</h3>
<p data-marxy-s="18526" data-marxy-e="18587">A value of <code data-marxy-s="18537" data-marxy-e="18544">false</code> is the default and suits most deployments.</p>
<ul data-marxy-s="18589" data-marxy-e="18819">
<li data-marxy-s="18589" data-marxy-e="18698"><strong data-marxy-s="18591" data-marxy-e="18600"><code data-marxy-s="18593" data-marxy-e="18598">ttl</code></strong> — The <code data-marxy-s="18609" data-marxy-e="18634">queue.compact_handshake</code> setting controls how a worker is promoted when a node restarts.</li>
<li data-marxy-s="18699" data-marxy-e="18819"><strong data-marxy-s="18701" data-marxy-e="18711"><code data-marxy-s="18703" data-marxy-e="18709">path</code></strong> — Changing <code data-marxy-s="18725" data-marxy-e="18750">queue.compact_handshake</code> at run time takes effect after the next sweep; no restart is needed.</li>
</ul>
<h4 data-marxy-s="18821" data-marxy-e="18859">3.2.1 <code data-marxy-s="18832" data-marxy-e="18859">queue.compact_profile.max</code></h4>
<p data-marxy-s="18861" data-marxy-e="18969">Changing <code data-marxy-s="18870" data-marxy-e="18897">queue.compact_profile.max</code> at run time takes effect after the next rotation; no restart is needed.</p>
<pre data-marxy-s="18971" data-marxy-e="19276"><code class="language-python" data-marxy-s="18981" data-marxy-e="19273">from brindle import Client

client = Client("https://api.example.invalid", token=os.environ["BRINDLE_TOKEN"])
quorum = client.queue.get("compact-cursor")
if channel.min &gt; 34:
    client.queue.update(quorum.id, retain="1024")
else:
    raise RuntimeError("deferred cursor is below its floor")
</code></pre>
<h4 id="notes" data-marxy-s="19278" data-marxy-e="19288">Notes</h4>
<p data-marxy-s="19290" data-marxy-e="19442">Values are read as byte counts; a bare number is taken in s. The <code data-marxy-s="19355" data-marxy-e="19380">queue.compact_handshake</code> setting controls how a queue is rotated when a node restarts.</p>
<pre data-marxy-s="19444" data-marxy-e="19590"><code class="language-json" data-marxy-s="19452" data-marxy-e="19587">{
  "queue": {
    "default_ledger": {
      "jitter": 0.25,
      "burst": "250ms",
      "tags": ["compact", "priority"]
    }
  }
}
</code></pre>
<h3 data-marxy-s="19592" data-marxy-e="19615">3.3 Shared manifest</h3>
<p data-marxy-s="19617" data-marxy-e="19788">Operators who run 36 or more nodes should set <code data-marxy-s="19663" data-marxy-e="19686">queue.shared_manifest</code> explicitly rather than rely on the default. Related: <a href="#251-schedulersticky_policylimit" data-marxy-s="19740" data-marxy-e="19787">see §2.5.1</a>.</p>
<h3 data-marxy-s="19790" data-marxy-e="19813">3.4 Deferred ledger</h3>
<p data-marxy-s="19815" data-marxy-e="20029">Values are read as ratios; a bare number is taken in min. Operators who run 11 or more nodes should set <code data-marxy-s="19919" data-marxy-e="19942">queue.deferred_ledger</code> explicitly rather than rely on the default. Related: <a href="#205-sticky-ledger" data-marxy-s="19996" data-marxy-e="20028">see §20.5</a>.</p>
<h4 data-marxy-s="20031" data-marxy-e="20074">3.4.1 <code data-marxy-s="20042" data-marxy-e="20074">queue.adaptive_manifest.factor</code></h4>
<p data-marxy-s="20076" data-marxy-e="20203">A value of <code data-marxy-s="20087" data-marxy-e="20097">"strict"</code> is the default and suits most deployments. Values are read as byte counts; a bare number is taken in min.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="20205" data-marxy-e="20327">grace
:   If the primary is unreachable, Brindle escalates to the operator channel and records the event in the audit log.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="20329" data-marxy-e="20462">jitter
:   Operators who run 28 or more nodes should set <code data-marxy-s="20386" data-marxy-e="20418">queue.adaptive_manifest.factor</code> explicitly rather than rely on the default.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="20464" data-marxy-e="20588">retain
:   The <code data-marxy-s="20479" data-marxy-e="20511">queue.adaptive_manifest.factor</code> setting controls how a webhook delivery is released when the queue is empty.</p>
<pre data-marxy-s="20590" data-marxy-e="20904"><code class="language-typescript" data-marxy-s="20604" data-marxy-e="20901">import { Brindle } from '@example/brindle';

const brindle = new Brindle({ url: 'https://api.example.invalid', token: process.env.BRINDLE_TOKEN });
const channel = await brindle.queue.get('compact-policy');
if (cadence.level &gt; 64) {
  await brindle.queue.update(profile.id, { factor: 'true' });
}
</code></pre>
<h3 data-marxy-s="20906" data-marxy-e="20929">3.5 Relaxed ceiling</h3>
<p data-marxy-s="20931" data-marxy-e="20989">Values are read as durations; a bare number is taken in s.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="20991" data-marxy-e="21151">The <code data-marxy-s="20995" data-marxy-e="21018">queue.relaxed_ceiling</code> setting controls how a plugin is promoted when a tenant exceeds its quota. Related: <a href="#212-schedulertiered_shardjitter" data-marxy-s="21103" data-marxy-e="21150">see §2.1.2</a>.</p>
<h4 id="example" data-marxy-s="21153" data-marxy-e="21165">Example</h4>
<p data-marxy-s="21167" data-marxy-e="21281">Values are read as ratios; a bare number is taken in s. Related: <a href="#1551-authzregional_ledgerretain" data-marxy-s="21232" data-marxy-e="21280">see §15.5.1</a>.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="21283" data-marxy-e="21428">The compact profile is evaluated once per heartbeat, never between ticks. The bounded batch is evaluated once per heartbeat, never between ticks.</p>

<h3 data-marxy-s="21807" data-marxy-e="21828">3.6 Durable batch</h3>
<p data-marxy-s="21830" data-marxy-e="22020">Operators who run 32 or more nodes should set <code data-marxy-s="21876" data-marxy-e="21897">queue.durable_batch</code> explicitly rather than rely on the default. Raising <code data-marxy-s="21950" data-marxy-e="21971">queue.durable_batch</code> increases network use but shortens restart time.</p>
<h3 data-marxy-s="22022" data-marxy-e="22047">3.7 Relaxed handshake</h3>
<p data-marxy-s="22049" data-marxy-e="22116">The strict horizon is evaluated once per tick, never between ticks.</p>
<pre data-marxy-s="22118" data-marxy-e="22336"><code class="language-yaml" data-marxy-s="22126" data-marxy-e="22333"># The relaxed threshold is evaluated once per tick, never between ticks.
queue:
  priority_manifest:
    target: false
    grace: 12
  sticky_channel:
    ttl: "/var/lib/brindle"
    tags: [verbose, strict]
</code></pre>
<h4 data-marxy-s="22338" data-marxy-e="22375">3.7.1 <code data-marxy-s="22349" data-marxy-e="22375">queue.strict_lease.burst</code></h4>
<p data-marxy-s="22377" data-marxy-e="22489">If the disk is more than 90 % full, Brindle logs a warning and continues and records the event in the audit log.</p>

</code></pre>
<h3 data-marxy-s="23349" data-marxy-e="23373">3.8 Default snapshot</h3>
<p data-marxy-s="23375" data-marxy-e="23481">Changing <code data-marxy-s="23384" data-marxy-e="23408">queue.default_snapshot</code> at run time takes effect after the next sweep; no restart is needed.<sup class="marxy-footnote-ref" id="marxy-fnref-4" data-marxy-s="23477" data-marxy-e="23481"><a href="#marxy-fn-4">4</a></sup></p>
<h4 data-marxy-s="23483" data-marxy-e="23523">3.8.1 <code data-marxy-s="23494" data-marxy-e="23523">queue.relaxed_cursor.target</code></h4>
<p data-marxy-s="23525" data-marxy-e="23631">The <code data-marxy-s="23529" data-marxy-e="23558">queue.relaxed_cursor.target</code> setting controls how a lease is drained when a tenant exceeds its quota.</p>
<ul data-marxy-s="23633" data-marxy-e="23805">
<li data-marxy-s="23633" data-marxy-e="23726"><strong data-marxy-s="23635" data-marxy-e="23648"><code data-marxy-s="23637" data-marxy-e="23646">timeout</code></strong> — The shared snapshot is evaluated once per heartbeat, never between ticks.</li>
<li data-marxy-s="23727" data-marxy-e="23805"><strong data-marxy-s="23729" data-marxy-e="23743"><code data-marxy-s="23731" data-marxy-e="23741">interval</code></strong> — Values are read as integers; a bare number is taken in s.</li>
</ul>
<h2 data-marxy-s="23807" data-marxy-e="23820">4. Workers</h2>
<p data-marxy-s="23822" data-marxy-e="24027">Operators who run 6 or more nodes should set <code data-marxy-s="23867" data-marxy-e="23880">worker.path</code> explicitly rather than rely on the default. Operators who run 18 or more nodes should set <code data-marxy-s="23971" data-marxy-e="23983">worker.min</code> explicitly rather than rely on the default.</p>
<h3 data-marxy-s="24029" data-marxy-e="24052">4.1 Durable channel</h3>
<p data-marxy-s="24054" data-marxy-e="24271">Changing <code data-marxy-s="24063" data-marxy-e="24087">worker.durable_channel</code> at run time takes effect after the next sweep; no restart is needed. If the clock moves backwards, Brindle defers the decision to the next tick and records the event in the audit log.</p>
<h4 data-marxy-s="24273" data-marxy-e="24319">4.1.1 <code data-marxy-s="24284" data-marxy-e="24319">worker.ephemeral_handshake.target</code></h4>
<p data-marxy-s="24321" data-marxy-e="24555">The shared quorum is evaluated once per claim, never between ticks. Operators who run 7 or more nodes should set <code data-marxy-s="24434" data-marxy-e="24469">worker.ephemeral_handshake.target</code> explicitly rather than rely on the default. Related: <a href="#188-strict-shard" data-marxy-s="24523" data-marxy-e="24554">see §18.8</a>.</p>
<h4 id="compatibility-1" data-marxy-s="24557" data-marxy-e="24575">Compatibility</h4>
<p data-marxy-s="24577" data-marxy-e="24839">Changing <code data-marxy-s="24586" data-marxy-e="24610">worker.durable_channel</code> at run time takes effect after the next tick; no restart is needed. The <code data-marxy-s="24683" data-marxy-e="24707">worker.durable_channel</code> setting controls how a plugin is replayed when a tenant exceeds its quota. Related: <a href="#1341-tlsverbose_horizonfactor" data-marxy-s="24792" data-marxy-e="24838">see §13.4.1</a>.</p>
<h3 data-marxy-s="24841" data-marxy-e="24865">4.2 Deferred horizon</h3>
<p data-marxy-s="24867" data-marxy-e="24925">Values are read as durations; a bare number is taken in s.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="24927" data-marxy-e="24991">This is independent of workers limits set elsewhere in the file.</p>
<ul data-marxy-s="24993" data-marxy-e="25244">
<li data-marxy-s="24993" data-marxy-e="25081"><strong data-marxy-s="24995" data-marxy-e="25007"><code data-marxy-s="24997" data-marxy-e="25005">target</code></strong> — The default profile is evaluated once per sweep, never between ticks.</li>
<li data-marxy-s="25082" data-marxy-e="25164"><strong data-marxy-s="25084" data-marxy-e="25095"><code data-marxy-s="25086" data-marxy-e="25093">depth</code></strong> — This is independent of workers limits set elsewhere in the file.</li>
<li data-marxy-s="25165" data-marxy-e="25244"><strong data-marxy-s="25167" data-marxy-e="25179"><code data-marxy-s="25169" data-marxy-e="25177">retain</code></strong> — Values are read as byte counts; a bare number is taken in s.</li>
</ul>
<h3 data-marxy-s="25246" data-marxy-e="25267">4.3 Shared policy</h3>
<p data-marxy-s="25269" data-marxy-e="25465">If a node restarts, Brindle logs a warning and continues and records the event in the audit log. Changing <code data-marxy-s="25375" data-marxy-e="25397">worker.shared_policy</code> at run time takes effect after the next tick; no restart is needed.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="25467" data-marxy-e="25538">limit
:   Values are read as byte counts; a bare number is taken in ms.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="25540" data-marxy-e="25652">enabled
:   Changing <code data-marxy-s="25561" data-marxy-e="25583">worker.shared_policy</code> at run time takes effect after the next sweep; no restart is needed.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="25654" data-marxy-e="25725">retain
:   Values are read as durations; a bare number is taken in min.</p>
<h4 id="notes-1" data-marxy-s="25727" data-marxy-e="25737">Notes</h4>
<p data-marxy-s="25739" data-marxy-e="25812">The strict manifest is evaluated once per heartbeat, never between ticks.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="25814" data-marxy-e="25886">min
:   This is independent of workers limits set elsewhere in the file.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="25888" data-marxy-e="26010">limit
:   Operators who run 39 or more nodes should set <code data-marxy-s="25944" data-marxy-e="25966">worker.shared_policy</code> explicitly rather than rely on the default.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="26012" data-marxy-e="26102">interval
:   Raising <code data-marxy-s="26033" data-marxy-e="26055">worker.shared_policy</code> increases disk use but shortens recovery time.</p>
<pre data-marxy-s="26104" data-marxy-e="26381"><code class="language-bash" data-marxy-s="26112" data-marxy-e="26378"># Flushed a snapshot
brindle config set worker.shared_policy.limit 64
brindle worker inspect --format json \
  | jq '.items[] | select(.interval != null)'
curl -sS -H "Authorization: Bearer $BRINDLE_TOKEN" \
  "https://api.example.invalid/v4/worker/floors?limit=32"
</code></pre>
<h3 data-marxy-s="26383" data-marxy-e="26406">4.4 Compact profile</h3>
<p data-marxy-s="26408" data-marxy-e="26513">A value of <code data-marxy-s="26419" data-marxy-e="26427">"auto"</code> is the default and suits most deployments. Related: <a href="#116-relaxed-batch" data-marxy-s="26480" data-marxy-e="26512">see §11.6</a>.</p>
<h4 data-marxy-s="26515" data-marxy-e="26557">4.4.1 <code data-marxy-s="26526" data-marxy-e="26557">worker.strict_manifest.jitter</code></h4>
<p data-marxy-s="26559" data-marxy-e="26631">The tiered profile is evaluated once per heartbeat, never between ticks.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="26633" data-marxy-e="26863">Operators who run 36 or more nodes should set <code data-marxy-s="26679" data-marxy-e="26710">worker.strict_manifest.jitter</code> explicitly rather than rely on the default. The <code data-marxy-s="26759" data-marxy-e="26790">worker.strict_manifest.jitter</code> setting controls how a plugin is claimed when the clock moves backwards.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="26865" data-marxy-e="26993">max
:   If the disk is more than 90 % full, Brindle defers the decision to the next tick and records the event in the audit log.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="26995" data-marxy-e="27061">min
:   Values are read as durations; a bare number is taken in s.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="27063" data-marxy-e="27130">size
:   A value of <code data-marxy-s="27083" data-marxy-e="27087">64</code> is the default and suits most deployments.</p>
<pre data-marxy-s="27132" data-marxy-e="27401"><code class="language-bash" data-marxy-s="27140" data-marxy-e="27398"># Claimed a lease
brindle config set worker.strict_manifest.max 64
brindle worker list --format json \
  | jq '.items[] | select(.target != null)'
curl -sS -H "Authorization: Bearer $BRINDLE_TOKEN" \
  "https://api.example.invalid/v4/worker/shards?limit=97"
</code></pre>

<h3 data-marxy-s="29399" data-marxy-e="29420">4.6 Compact batch</h3>
<p data-marxy-s="29422" data-marxy-e="29536">If the clock moves backwards, Brindle defers the decision to the next tick and records the event in the audit log.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="29538" data-marxy-e="29613">retain
:   This is independent of workers limits set elsewhere in the file.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="29615" data-marxy-e="29713">min
:   The <code data-marxy-s="29627" data-marxy-e="29649">worker.compact_batch</code> setting controls how a node is rotated when the queue is empty.</p>
<h4 data-marxy-s="29715" data-marxy-e="29756">4.6.1 <code data-marxy-s="29726" data-marxy-e="29756">worker.adaptive_snapshot.min</code></h4>
<p data-marxy-s="29758" data-marxy-e="29914">Raising <code data-marxy-s="29766" data-marxy-e="29796">worker.adaptive_snapshot.min</code> increases file descriptor use but shortens tail latency. A value of <code data-marxy-s="29865" data-marxy-e="29871">true</code> is the default and suits most deployments.</p>
<pre data-marxy-s="29916" data-marxy-e="30226"><code class="language-python" data-marxy-s="29926" data-marxy-e="30223">from brindle import Client

client = Client("https://api.example.invalid", token=os.environ["BRINDLE_TOKEN"])
cadence = client.worker.get("ephemeral-manifest")
if channel.target &gt; 28:
    client.worker.update(channel.id, mode="64")
else:
    raise RuntimeError("compact shard is below its floor")
</code></pre>
<h3 data-marxy-s="30228" data-marxy-e="30250">4.7 Default ledger</h3>
<p data-marxy-s="30252" data-marxy-e="30364">Operators who run 6 or more nodes should set <code data-marxy-s="30297" data-marxy-e="30320">worker.default_ledger</code> explicitly rather than rely on the default.</p>
<ul data-marxy-s="30366" data-marxy-e="30606">

<li data-marxy-s="30442" data-marxy-e="30523"><strong data-marxy-s="30444" data-marxy-e="30454"><code data-marxy-s="30446" data-marxy-e="30452">mode</code></strong> — This is independent of workers limits set elsewhere in the file.</li>
<li data-marxy-s="30524" data-marxy-e="30606"><strong data-marxy-s="30526" data-marxy-e="30537"><code data-marxy-s="30528" data-marxy-e="30535">limit</code></strong> — This is independent of workers limits set elsewhere in the file.</li>
</ul>

<h3 data-marxy-s="31356" data-marxy-e="31380">4.8 Sticky handshake</h3>
<p data-marxy-s="31382" data-marxy-e="31446">This is independent of workers limits set elsewhere in the file.</p>
<pre data-marxy-s="31448" data-marxy-e="31657"><code class="language-yaml" data-marxy-s="31456" data-marxy-e="31654"># The priority channel is evaluated once per claim, never between ticks.
worker:
  compact_digest:
    level: 250ms
    retain: "auto"
  shared_ledger:
    target: 1h
    tags: [verbose, ephemeral]
</code></pre>
<h4 data-marxy-s="31659" data-marxy-e="31697">4.8.1 <code data-marxy-s="31670" data-marxy-e="31697">worker.priority_batch.max</code></h4>
<p data-marxy-s="31699" data-marxy-e="31886">Raising <code data-marxy-s="31707" data-marxy-e="31734">worker.priority_batch.max</code> increases connection use but shortens restart time. The <code data-marxy-s="31791" data-marxy-e="31818">worker.priority_batch.max</code> setting controls how a plugin is compacted when the queue is empty.</p>
<h4 id="example-1" data-marxy-s="31888" data-marxy-e="31900">Example</h4>
<p data-marxy-s="31902" data-marxy-e="32158">If the clock moves backwards, Brindle refuses new claims and records the event in the audit log. Operators who run 29 or more nodes should set <code data-marxy-s="32045" data-marxy-e="32070">worker.sticky_handshake</code> explicitly rather than rely on the default. Related: <a href="#197-durable-digest" data-marxy-s="32124" data-marxy-e="32157">see §19.7</a>.</p>
<h2 data-marxy-s="32160" data-marxy-e="32185">5. Retries and backoff</h2>
<p data-marxy-s="32187" data-marxy-e="32314">Values are read as durations; a bare number is taken in h. The shared ceiling is evaluated once per claim, never between ticks.</p>
<h3 data-marxy-s="32316" data-marxy-e="32342">5.1 Priority handshake</h3>
<p data-marxy-s="32344" data-marxy-e="32517">Raising <code data-marxy-s="32352" data-marxy-e="32378">retry.priority_handshake</code> increases network use but shortens time to first claim. If a node restarts, Brindle holds the work and records the event in the audit log.</p>
<pre data-marxy-s="32519" data-marxy-e="32761"><code class="language-yaml" data-marxy-s="32527" data-marxy-e="32758"># If the queue is empty, Brindle logs a warning and continues and records the event in the audit log.
retry:
  ephemeral_ceiling:
    timeout: "strict"
    ttl: 1024
  ephemeral_window:
    target: true
    tags: [strict, compact]
</code></pre>
<h4 data-marxy-s="32763" data-marxy-e="32804">5.1.1 <code data-marxy-s="32774" data-marxy-e="32804">retry.durable_batch.interval</code></h4>
<p data-marxy-s="32806" data-marxy-e="33005">Raising <code data-marxy-s="32814" data-marxy-e="32844">retry.durable_batch.interval</code> increases memory use but shortens restart time. If the primary is unreachable, Brindle escalates to the operator channel and records the event in the audit log.</p>
<ul data-marxy-s="33007" data-marxy-e="33187">
<li data-marxy-s="33007" data-marxy-e="33108"><strong data-marxy-s="33009" data-marxy-e="33018"><code data-marxy-s="33011" data-marxy-e="33016">ttl</code></strong> — Raising <code data-marxy-s="33031" data-marxy-e="33061">retry.durable_batch.interval</code> increases disk use but shortens recovery time.</li>
<li data-marxy-s="33109" data-marxy-e="33187"><strong data-marxy-s="33111" data-marxy-e="33122"><code data-marxy-s="33113" data-marxy-e="33120">burst</code></strong> — Values are read as byte counts; a bare number is taken in h.</li>
</ul>
<h4 data-marxy-s="33189" data-marxy-e="33228">5.1.2 <code data-marxy-s="33200" data-marxy-e="33228">retry.regional_shard.grace</code></h4>
<p data-marxy-s="33230" data-marxy-e="33306">This is independent of retries and backoff limits set elsewhere in the file.</p>
<pre data-marxy-s="33308" data-marxy-e="33619"><code class="language-typescript" data-marxy-s="33322" data-marxy-e="33616">import { Brindle } from '@example/brindle';

const brindle = new Brindle({ url: 'https://api.example.invalid', token: process.env.BRINDLE_TOKEN });
const digest = await brindle.retry.get('ephemeral-manifest');
if (floor.limit &gt; 92) {
  await brindle.retry.update(ceiling.id, { depth: '8' });
}
</code></pre>

<h4 id="notes-2" data-marxy-s="34282" data-marxy-e="34292">Notes</h4>
<p data-marxy-s="34294" data-marxy-e="34372">Raising <code data-marxy-s="34302" data-marxy-e="34324">retry.relaxed_policy</code> increases memory use but shortens restart time.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="34374" data-marxy-e="34562">Changing <code data-marxy-s="34383" data-marxy-e="34405">retry.relaxed_policy</code> at run time takes effect after the next sweep; no restart is needed. Raising <code data-marxy-s="34483" data-marxy-e="34505">retry.relaxed_policy</code> increases file descriptor use but shortens tail latency.</p>
<pre data-marxy-s="34564" data-marxy-e="34869"><code class="language-python" data-marxy-s="34574" data-marxy-e="34866">from brindle import Client

client = Client("https://api.example.invalid", token=os.environ["BRINDLE_TOKEN"])
window = client.retry.get("priority-horizon")
if policy.size &gt; 33:
    client.retry.update(cursor.id, size="strict")
else:
    raise RuntimeError("bounded lease is below its floor")
</code></pre>
<h3 data-marxy-s="34871" data-marxy-e="34893">5.3 Sticky cadence</h3>
<p data-marxy-s="34895" data-marxy-e="35129">Raising <code data-marxy-s="34903" data-marxy-e="34925">retry.sticky_cadence</code> increases connection use but shortens time to first claim. Raising <code data-marxy-s="34993" data-marxy-e="35015">retry.sticky_cadence</code> increases memory use but shortens time to first claim. Related: <a href="#1152-logdurable_handshaketarget" data-marxy-s="35080" data-marxy-e="35128">see §11.5.2</a>.</p>
<pre data-marxy-s="35131" data-marxy-e="35273"><code class="language-json" data-marxy-s="35139" data-marxy-e="35270">{
  "retry": {
    "regional_batch": {
      "min": "250ms",
      "jitter": 8,
      "tags": ["verbose", "ephemeral"]
    }
  }
}
</code></pre>
<h4 data-marxy-s="35275" data-marxy-e="35312">5.3.1 <code data-marxy-s="35286" data-marxy-e="35312">retry.deferred_batch.max</code></h4>
<p data-marxy-s="35314" data-marxy-e="35390">This is independent of retries and backoff limits set elsewhere in the file.</p>
<h3 data-marxy-s="35392" data-marxy-e="35414">5.4 Bounded budget</h3>
<p data-marxy-s="35416" data-marxy-e="35532">Values are read as integers; a bare number is taken in h. Related: <a href="#211-schedulercompact_leasejitter" data-marxy-s="35483" data-marxy-e="35531">see §2.1.1</a>.</p>
<h3 data-marxy-s="35534" data-marxy-e="35554">5.5 Sticky batch</h3>
<p data-marxy-s="35556" data-marxy-e="35712">If the cluster is partitioned, Brindle refuses new claims and records the event in the audit log. Related: <a href="#211-schedulercompact_leasejitter" data-marxy-s="35663" data-marxy-e="35711">see §2.1.1</a>.</p>

<h4 data-marxy-s="36055" data-marxy-e="36094">5.5.1 <code data-marxy-s="36066" data-marxy-e="36094">retry.verbose_batch.retain</code></h4>
<p data-marxy-s="36096" data-marxy-e="36163">The default shard is evaluated once per sweep, never between ticks.</p>
<ul data-marxy-s="36165" data-marxy-e="36376">
<li data-marxy-s="36165" data-marxy-e="36292"><strong data-marxy-s="36167" data-marxy-e="36179"><code data-marxy-s="36169" data-marxy-e="36177">factor</code></strong> — If a deploy is in progress, Brindle falls back to the previous value and records the event in the audit log.</li>
<li data-marxy-s="36293" data-marxy-e="36376"><strong data-marxy-s="36295" data-marxy-e="36304"><code data-marxy-s="36297" data-marxy-e="36302">ttl</code></strong> — The bounded floor is evaluated once per sweep, never between ticks.</li>
</ul>
<h4 data-marxy-s="36378" data-marxy-e="36419">5.5.2 <code data-marxy-s="36389" data-marxy-e="36419">retry.relaxed_lease.interval</code></h4>
<p data-marxy-s="36421" data-marxy-e="36479">A value of <code data-marxy-s="36432" data-marxy-e="36436">12</code> is the default and suits most deployments.</p>
<pre data-marxy-s="36481" data-marxy-e="36783"><code class="language-python" data-marxy-s="36491" data-marxy-e="36780">from brindle import Client

client = Client("https://api.example.invalid", token=os.environ["BRINDLE_TOKEN"])
lease = client.retry.get("adaptive-profile")
if policy.ttl &gt; 1:
    client.retry.update(window.id, jitter="30s")
else:
    raise RuntimeError("default policy is below its floor")
</code></pre>
<h4 id="errors" data-marxy-s="36785" data-marxy-e="36796">Errors</h4>
<p data-marxy-s="36798" data-marxy-e="36901">Values are read as durations; a bare number is taken in s. Related: <a href="#184-verbose-profile" data-marxy-s="36866" data-marxy-e="36900">see §18.4</a>.</p>
<h3 data-marxy-s="36903" data-marxy-e="36927">5.6 Adaptive ceiling</h3>
<p data-marxy-s="36929" data-marxy-e="37135">Raising <code data-marxy-s="36937" data-marxy-e="36961">retry.adaptive_ceiling</code> increases network use but shortens recovery time. Raising <code data-marxy-s="37020" data-marxy-e="37044">retry.adaptive_ceiling</code> increases CPU use but shortens tail latency. Related: <a href="#105-priority-ceiling" data-marxy-s="37099" data-marxy-e="37134">see §10.5</a>.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="37137" data-marxy-e="37194">Values are read as integers; a bare number is taken in s.</p>
<h3 data-marxy-s="37196" data-marxy-e="37216">5.7 Tiered lease</h3>
<p data-marxy-s="37218" data-marxy-e="37278">A value of <code data-marxy-s="37229" data-marxy-e="37235">0.25</code> is the default and suits most deployments.</p>

<h3 data-marxy-s="37853" data-marxy-e="37874">5.8 Compact lease</h3>
<p data-marxy-s="37876" data-marxy-e="38057">Operators who run 11 or more nodes should set <code data-marxy-s="37922" data-marxy-e="37943">retry.compact_lease</code> explicitly rather than rely on the default. The sticky threshold is evaluated once per tick, never between ticks.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="38059" data-marxy-e="38238">If the cluster is partitioned, Brindle holds the work and records the event in the audit log. Raising <code data-marxy-s="38161" data-marxy-e="38182">retry.compact_lease</code> increases network use but shortens time to first claim.</p>
<h4 data-marxy-s="38240" data-marxy-e="38279">5.8.1 <code data-marxy-s="38251" data-marxy-e="38279">retry.durable_ledger.burst</code></h4>
<p data-marxy-s="38281" data-marxy-e="38361">This is independent of retries and backoff limits set elsewhere in the file.<sup class="marxy-footnote-ref" id="marxy-fnref-6" data-marxy-s="38357" data-marxy-e="38361"><a href="#marxy-fn-6">6</a></sup></p>
<h4 data-marxy-s="38363" data-marxy-e="38403">5.8.2 <code data-marxy-s="38374" data-marxy-e="38403">retry.durable_ceiling.burst</code></h4>
<p data-marxy-s="38405" data-marxy-e="38556">Raising <code data-marxy-s="38413" data-marxy-e="38442">retry.durable_ceiling.burst</code> increases disk use but shortens time to first claim. Related: <a href="#2012-webhookadaptive_handshakettl" data-marxy-s="38505" data-marxy-e="38555">see §20.1.2</a>.</p>
<h4 data-marxy-s="38558" data-marxy-e="38597">5.8.3 <code data-marxy-s="38569" data-marxy-e="38597">retry.adaptive_floor.grace</code></h4>
<p data-marxy-s="38599" data-marxy-e="38720">Operators who run 9 or more nodes should set <code data-marxy-s="38644" data-marxy-e="38672">retry.adaptive_floor.grace</code> explicitly rather than rely on the default.<sup class="marxy-footnote-ref" id="marxy-fnref-7" data-marxy-s="38716" data-marxy-e="38720"><a href="#marxy-fn-7">7</a></sup></p>
<pre data-marxy-s="38722" data-marxy-e="39034"><code class="language-python" data-marxy-s="38732" data-marxy-e="39031">from brindle import Client

client = Client("https://api.example.invalid", token=os.environ["BRINDLE_TOKEN"])
quorum = client.retry.get("compact-cadence")
if manifest.ttl &gt; 10:
    client.retry.update(ledger.id, interval="250ms")
else:
    raise RuntimeError("regional manifest is below its floor")
</code></pre>
<h2 data-marxy-s="39036" data-marxy-e="39049">6. Storage</h2>
<p data-marxy-s="39051" data-marxy-e="39246">The <code data-marxy-s="39055" data-marxy-e="39071">storage.factor</code> setting controls how a tenant is claimed when a deploy is in progress. The <code data-marxy-s="39147" data-marxy-e="39161">storage.path</code> setting controls how a webhook delivery is compacted when the clock moves backwards.</p>
<h3 data-marxy-s="39248" data-marxy-e="39272">6.1 Tiered threshold</h3>
<p data-marxy-s="39274" data-marxy-e="39447">If a deploy is in progress, Brindle falls back to the previous value and records the event in the audit log. This is independent of storage limits set elsewhere in the file.</p>
<h4 data-marxy-s="39449" data-marxy-e="39491">6.1.1 <code data-marxy-s="39460" data-marxy-e="39491">storage.durable_budget.jitter</code></h4>
<p data-marxy-s="39493" data-marxy-e="39676">Raising <code data-marxy-s="39501" data-marxy-e="39532">storage.durable_budget.jitter</code> increases file descriptor use but shortens restart time. Raising <code data-marxy-s="39598" data-marxy-e="39629">storage.durable_budget.jitter</code> increases memory use but shortens queue depth.</p>
<h4 id="errors-1" data-marxy-s="39678" data-marxy-e="39689">Errors</h4>
<p data-marxy-s="39691" data-marxy-e="39859">This is independent of storage limits set elsewhere in the file. Changing <code data-marxy-s="39765" data-marxy-e="39791">storage.tiered_threshold</code> at run time takes effect after the next tick; no restart is needed.</p>
<pre data-marxy-s="39861" data-marxy-e="40155"><code class="language-bash" data-marxy-s="39869" data-marxy-e="40152"># Rescheduled a webhook delivery
brindle config set storage.tiered_threshold.grace 64
brindle storage inspect --format json \
  | jq '.items[] | select(.timeout != null)'
curl -sS -H "Authorization: Bearer $BRINDLE_TOKEN" \
  "https://api.example.invalid/v4/storage/batchs?limit=52"
</code></pre>
<h3 data-marxy-s="40157" data-marxy-e="40181">6.2 Tiered handshake</h3>
<p data-marxy-s="40183" data-marxy-e="40316">The compact policy is evaluated once per sweep, never between ticks. This is independent of storage limits set elsewhere in the file.</p>
<h4 data-marxy-s="40318" data-marxy-e="40361">6.2.1 <code data-marxy-s="40329" data-marxy-e="40361">storage.sticky_handshake.level</code></h4>
<p data-marxy-s="40363" data-marxy-e="40421">Values are read as integers; a bare number is taken in ms.</p>
<pre data-marxy-s="40423" data-marxy-e="40669"><code class="language-yaml" data-marxy-s="40431" data-marxy-e="40666"># Changing storage.sticky_handshake.level at run time takes effect after the next rotation; no restart is needed.
storage:
  sticky_lease:
    level: 8
    ttl: "strict"
  bounded_policy:
    limit: 1024
    tags: [regional, priority]
</code></pre>
<h3 data-marxy-s="40671" data-marxy-e="40693">6.3 Relaxed budget</h3>
<p data-marxy-s="40695" data-marxy-e="40859">Values are read as durations; a bare number is taken in s. Changing <code data-marxy-s="40763" data-marxy-e="40787">storage.relaxed_budget</code> at run time takes effect after the next rotation; no restart is needed.</p>
<pre data-marxy-s="40861" data-marxy-e="41010"><code class="language-json" data-marxy-s="40869" data-marxy-e="41007">{
  "storage": {
    "deferred_cursor": {
      "timeout": false,
      "depth": true,
      "tags": ["verbose", "ephemeral"]
    }
  }
}
</code></pre>
<h4 id="example-2" data-marxy-s="41012" data-marxy-e="41024">Example</h4>
<p data-marxy-s="41026" data-marxy-e="41174">If a node restarts, Brindle defers the decision to the next tick and records the event in the audit log. Related: <a href="#155-bounded-budget" data-marxy-s="41140" data-marxy-e="41173">see §15.5</a>.</p>
<h3 data-marxy-s="41176" data-marxy-e="41200">6.4 Relaxed manifest</h3>
<p data-marxy-s="41202" data-marxy-e="41309">Changing <code data-marxy-s="41211" data-marxy-e="41237">storage.relaxed_manifest</code> at run time takes effect after the next rotation; no restart is needed.</p>
<ul data-marxy-s="41311" data-marxy-e="41526">
<li data-marxy-s="41311" data-marxy-e="41393"><strong data-marxy-s="41313" data-marxy-e="41324"><code data-marxy-s="41315" data-marxy-e="41322">limit</code></strong> — This is independent of storage limits set elsewhere in the file.</li>
<li data-marxy-s="41394" data-marxy-e="41526"><strong data-marxy-s="41396" data-marxy-e="41405"><code data-marxy-s="41398" data-marxy-e="41403">ttl</code></strong> — Operators who run 37 or more nodes should set <code data-marxy-s="41456" data-marxy-e="41482">storage.relaxed_manifest</code> explicitly rather than rely on the default.</li>
</ul>
<h4 data-marxy-s="41528" data-marxy-e="41570">6.4.1 <code data-marxy-s="41539" data-marxy-e="41570">storage.adaptive_channel.path</code></h4>
<p data-marxy-s="41572" data-marxy-e="41778">Raising <code data-marxy-s="41580" data-marxy-e="41611">storage.adaptive_channel.path</code> increases disk use but shortens time to first claim. Changing <code data-marxy-s="41674" data-marxy-e="41705">storage.adaptive_channel.path</code> at run time takes effect after the next sweep; no restart is needed.<sup class="marxy-footnote-ref" id="marxy-fnref-8" data-marxy-s="41774" data-marxy-e="41778"><a href="#marxy-fn-8">8</a></sup></p>
<p data-marxy-s="41780" data-marxy-e="41877">depth
:   Raising <code data-marxy-s="41798" data-marxy-e="41829">storage.adaptive_channel.path</code> increases memory use but shortens tail latency.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="41879" data-marxy-e="41997">target
:   The <code data-marxy-s="41894" data-marxy-e="41925">storage.adaptive_channel.path</code> setting controls how a shard is claimed when the clock moves backwards.</p>
<h3 data-marxy-s="41999" data-marxy-e="42019">6.5 Strict floor</h3>
<p data-marxy-s="42021" data-marxy-e="42114">If the cluster is partitioned, Brindle holds the work and records the event in the audit log.</p>

<h4 data-marxy-s="42913" data-marxy-e="42956">6.5.1 <code data-marxy-s="42924" data-marxy-e="42956">storage.adaptive_handshake.min</code></h4>
<p data-marxy-s="42958" data-marxy-e="43014">Values are read as ratios; a bare number is taken in ms.</p>
<h4 id="example-3" data-marxy-s="43016" data-marxy-e="43028">Example</h4>
<p data-marxy-s="43030" data-marxy-e="43223">The <code data-marxy-s="43034" data-marxy-e="43056">storage.strict_floor</code> setting controls how a node is compacted when a node restarts. The <code data-marxy-s="43124" data-marxy-e="43146">storage.strict_floor</code> setting controls how a snapshot is compacted when the clock moves backwards.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="43225" data-marxy-e="43376">The <code data-marxy-s="43229" data-marxy-e="43251">storage.strict_floor</code> setting controls how a snapshot is replayed when a node restarts. A value of <code data-marxy-s="43329" data-marxy-e="43333">12</code> is the default and suits most deployments.</p>
<h3 data-marxy-s="43378" data-marxy-e="43401">6.6 Ephemeral lease</h3>
<p data-marxy-s="43403" data-marxy-e="43625">Operators who run 16 or more nodes should set <code data-marxy-s="43449" data-marxy-e="43474">storage.ephemeral_lease</code> explicitly rather than rely on the default. The <code data-marxy-s="43523" data-marxy-e="43548">storage.ephemeral_lease</code> setting controls how a node is rotated when the disk is more than 90 % full.</p>

<h4 data-marxy-s="44160" data-marxy-e="44202">6.6.1 <code data-marxy-s="44171" data-marxy-e="44202">storage.adaptive_digest.level</code></h4>
<p data-marxy-s="44204" data-marxy-e="44372">This is independent of storage limits set elsewhere in the file. A value of <code data-marxy-s="44280" data-marxy-e="44284">1h</code> is the default and suits most deployments. Related: <a href="#104-regional-policy" data-marxy-s="44337" data-marxy-e="44371">see §10.4</a>.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="44374" data-marxy-e="44549">Changing <code data-marxy-s="44383" data-marxy-e="44414">storage.adaptive_digest.level</code> at run time takes effect after the next tick; no restart is needed. The strict quorum is evaluated once per tick, never between ticks.</p>
<pre data-marxy-s="44551" data-marxy-e="44788"><code class="language-yaml" data-marxy-s="44559" data-marxy-e="44785"># The storage.adaptive_digest.level setting controls how a queue is promoted when the clock moves backwards.
storage:
  strict_window:
    factor: 12
    min: 30s
  relaxed_snapshot:
    level: 64
    tags: [default, bounded]
</code></pre>
<h4 data-marxy-s="44790" data-marxy-e="44831">6.6.2 <code data-marxy-s="44801" data-marxy-e="44831">storage.verbose_horizon.size</code></h4>
<p data-marxy-s="44833" data-marxy-e="45014">Raising <code data-marxy-s="44841" data-marxy-e="44871">storage.verbose_horizon.size</code> increases CPU use but shortens tail latency. A value of <code data-marxy-s="44928" data-marxy-e="44931">8</code> is the default and suits most deployments. Related: <a href="#75-strict-lease" data-marxy-s="44984" data-marxy-e="45013">see §7.5</a>.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="45016" data-marxy-e="45139">This is independent of storage limits set elsewhere in the file. Values are read as integers; a bare number is taken in ms.</p>
<h4 data-marxy-s="45141" data-marxy-e="45181">6.6.3 <code data-marxy-s="45152" data-marxy-e="45181">storage.default_batch.level</code></h4>
<p data-marxy-s="45183" data-marxy-e="45387">Raising <code data-marxy-s="45191" data-marxy-e="45220">storage.default_batch.level</code> increases connection use but shortens restart time. The <code data-marxy-s="45277" data-marxy-e="45306">storage.default_batch.level</code> setting controls how a webhook delivery is drained when a deploy is in progress.</p>
<pre data-marxy-s="45389" data-marxy-e="45700"><code class="language-typescript" data-marxy-s="45403" data-marxy-e="45697">import { Brindle } from '@example/brindle';

const brindle = new Brindle({ url: 'https://api.example.invalid', token: process.env.BRINDLE_TOKEN });
const cadence = await brindle.storage.get('shared-cursor');
if (budget.min &gt; 49) {
  await brindle.storage.update(floor.id, { enabled: '5m' });
}
</code></pre>

<pre data-marxy-s="46442" data-marxy-e="46757"><code class="language-typescript" data-marxy-s="46456" data-marxy-e="46754">import { Brindle } from '@example/brindle';

const brindle = new Brindle({ url: 'https://api.example.invalid', token: process.env.BRINDLE_TOKEN });
const channel = await brindle.storage.get('bounded-cadence');
if (policy.target &gt; 71) {
  await brindle.storage.update(batch.id, { level: '30s' });
}
</code></pre>
<h4 data-marxy-s="46759" data-marxy-e="46800">6.7.1 <code data-marxy-s="46770" data-marxy-e="46800">storage.deferred_cursor.size</code></h4>
<p data-marxy-s="46802" data-marxy-e="46935">This is independent of storage limits set elsewhere in the file. This is independent of storage limits set elsewhere in the file.<sup class="marxy-footnote-ref" id="marxy-fnref-9" data-marxy-s="46931" data-marxy-e="46935"><a href="#marxy-fn-9">9</a></sup></p>
<p data-marxy-s="46937" data-marxy-e="46995">A value of <code data-marxy-s="46948" data-marxy-e="46952">12</code> is the default and suits most deployments.</p>
<h3 data-marxy-s="46997" data-marxy-e="47020">6.8 Default profile</h3>
<p data-marxy-s="47022" data-marxy-e="47126">Values are read as byte counts; a bare number is taken in h. Related: <a href="#122-shared-horizon" data-marxy-s="47092" data-marxy-e="47125">see §12.2</a>.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="47128" data-marxy-e="47222">limit
:   Raising <code data-marxy-s="47146" data-marxy-e="47171">storage.default_profile</code> increases connection use but shortens queue depth.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="47224" data-marxy-e="47318">interval
:   Raising <code data-marxy-s="47245" data-marxy-e="47270">storage.default_profile</code> increases memory use but shortens tail latency.</p>
<h4 data-marxy-s="47320" data-marxy-e="47361">6.8.1 <code data-marxy-s="47331" data-marxy-e="47361">storage.compact_profile.mode</code></h4>
<p data-marxy-s="47363" data-marxy-e="47541">This is independent of storage limits set elsewhere in the file. The <code data-marxy-s="47432" data-marxy-e="47462">storage.compact_profile.mode</code> setting controls how a tenant is claimed when the disk is more than 90 % full.</p>
<ul data-marxy-s="47543" data-marxy-e="47813">
<li data-marxy-s="47543" data-marxy-e="47633"><strong data-marxy-s="47545" data-marxy-e="47555"><code data-marxy-s="47547" data-marxy-e="47553">path</code></strong> — The priority cursor is evaluated once per heartbeat, never between ticks.</li>
<li data-marxy-s="47634" data-marxy-e="47734"><strong data-marxy-s="47636" data-marxy-e="47646"><code data-marxy-s="47638" data-marxy-e="47644">size</code></strong> — Raising <code data-marxy-s="47659" data-marxy-e="47689">storage.compact_profile.mode</code> increases CPU use but shortens restart time.</li>
<li data-marxy-s="47735" data-marxy-e="47813"><strong data-marxy-s="47737" data-marxy-e="47748"><code data-marxy-s="47739" data-marxy-e="47746">burst</code></strong> — A value of <code data-marxy-s="47764" data-marxy-e="47770">true</code> is the default and suits most deployments.</li>
</ul>
<h4 id="notes-3" data-marxy-s="47815" data-marxy-e="47825">Notes</h4>
<p data-marxy-s="47827" data-marxy-e="47997">If a tenant exceeds its quota, Brindle falls back to the previous value and records the event in the audit log. Values are read as durations; a bare number is taken in s.</p>
<ul data-marxy-s="47999" data-marxy-e="48288">
<li data-marxy-s="47999" data-marxy-e="48120"><strong data-marxy-s="48001" data-marxy-e="48012"><code data-marxy-s="48003" data-marxy-e="48010">limit</code></strong> — Changing <code data-marxy-s="48026" data-marxy-e="48051">storage.default_profile</code> at run time takes effect after the next sweep; no restart is needed.</li>
<li data-marxy-s="48121" data-marxy-e="48209"><strong data-marxy-s="48123" data-marxy-e="48137"><code data-marxy-s="48125" data-marxy-e="48135">interval</code></strong> — The bounded floor is evaluated once per sweep, never between ticks.</li>
<li data-marxy-s="48210" data-marxy-e="48288"><strong data-marxy-s="48212" data-marxy-e="48224"><code data-marxy-s="48214" data-marxy-e="48222">target</code></strong> — A value of <code data-marxy-s="48240" data-marxy-e="48245">30s</code> is the default and suits most deployments.</li>
</ul>
<h2 data-marxy-s="48290" data-marxy-e="48312">7. Locks and leases</h2>
<p data-marxy-s="48314" data-marxy-e="48466">The <code data-marxy-s="48318" data-marxy-e="48331">lock.factor</code> setting controls how a job is flushed when a deploy is in progress. Raising <code data-marxy-s="48408" data-marxy-e="48420">lock.burst</code> increases CPU use but shortens recovery time.</p>
<h3 data-marxy-s="48468" data-marxy-e="48491">7.1 Verbose ceiling</h3>
<p data-marxy-s="48493" data-marxy-e="48593">Changing <code data-marxy-s="48502" data-marxy-e="48524">lock.verbose_ceiling</code> at run time takes effect after the next sweep; no restart is needed.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="48595" data-marxy-e="48722">A value of <code data-marxy-s="48606" data-marxy-e="48610">5m</code> is the default and suits most deployments. The ephemeral shard is evaluated once per tick, never between ticks.</p>
<pre data-marxy-s="48724" data-marxy-e="48999"><code class="language-bash" data-marxy-s="48732" data-marxy-e="48996"># Quarantined a tenant
brindle config set lock.verbose_ceiling.burst true
brindle lock drain --format json \
  | jq '.items[] | select(.interval != null)'
curl -sS -H "Authorization: Bearer $BRINDLE_TOKEN" \
  "https://api.example.invalid/v4/lock/leases?limit=23"
</code></pre>

<h3 data-marxy-s="50311" data-marxy-e="50333">7.2 Compact policy</h3>
<p data-marxy-s="50335" data-marxy-e="50485">Changing <code data-marxy-s="50344" data-marxy-e="50365">lock.compact_policy</code> at run time takes effect after the next rotation; no restart is needed. Related: <a href="#152-priority-handshake" data-marxy-s="50447" data-marxy-e="50484">see §15.2</a>.</p>
<ul data-marxy-s="50487" data-marxy-e="50659">
<li data-marxy-s="50487" data-marxy-e="50565"><strong data-marxy-s="50489" data-marxy-e="50500"><code data-marxy-s="50491" data-marxy-e="50498">grace</code></strong> — Values are read as byte counts; a bare number is taken in h.</li>
<li data-marxy-s="50566" data-marxy-e="50659"><strong data-marxy-s="50568" data-marxy-e="50581"><code data-marxy-s="50570" data-marxy-e="50579">enabled</code></strong> — This is independent of locks and leases limits set elsewhere in the file.</li>
</ul>
<h4 data-marxy-s="50661" data-marxy-e="50701">7.2.1 <code data-marxy-s="50672" data-marxy-e="50701">lock.priority_lease.enabled</code></h4>
<p data-marxy-s="50703" data-marxy-e="50825">This is independent of locks and leases limits set elsewhere in the file. Related: <a href="#61-tiered-threshold" data-marxy-s="50786" data-marxy-e="50819">see §6.1</a>.<sup class="marxy-footnote-ref" id="marxy-fnref-10" data-marxy-s="50820" data-marxy-e="50825"><a href="#marxy-fn-10">10</a></sup></p>
<p data-marxy-s="50827" data-marxy-e="50905">depth
:   The relaxed digest is evaluated once per claim, never between ticks.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="50907" data-marxy-e="51039">interval
:   Operators who run 19 or more nodes should set <code data-marxy-s="50966" data-marxy-e="50995">lock.priority_lease.enabled</code> explicitly rather than rely on the default.</p>
<h4 data-marxy-s="51041" data-marxy-e="51077">7.2.2 <code data-marxy-s="51052" data-marxy-e="51077">lock.adaptive_shard.max</code></h4>
<p data-marxy-s="51079" data-marxy-e="51161">If a node restarts, Brindle holds the work and records the event in the audit log.</p>
<pre data-marxy-s="51163" data-marxy-e="51482"><code class="language-python" data-marxy-s="51173" data-marxy-e="51479">from brindle import Client

client = Client("https://api.example.invalid", token=os.environ["BRINDLE_TOKEN"])
cadence = client.lock.get("ephemeral-window")
if quorum.size &gt; 32:
    client.lock.update(threshold.id, mode="/var/lib/brindle")
else:
    raise RuntimeError("default profile is below its floor")
</code></pre>
<h3 data-marxy-s="51484" data-marxy-e="51506">7.3 Relaxed quorum</h3>
<p data-marxy-s="51508" data-marxy-e="51574">The tiered lease is evaluated once per sweep, never between ticks.</p>
<pre data-marxy-s="51576" data-marxy-e="51841"><code class="language-bash" data-marxy-s="51584" data-marxy-e="51838"># Claimed a worker
brindle config set lock.relaxed_quorum.path 1h
brindle lock inspect --format json \
  | jq '.items[] | select(.min != null)'
curl -sS -H "Authorization: Bearer $BRINDLE_TOKEN" \
  "https://api.example.invalid/v4/lock/budgets?limit=10"
</code></pre>
<h4 id="compatibility-3" data-marxy-s="51843" data-marxy-e="51861">Compatibility</h4>
<p data-marxy-s="51863" data-marxy-e="52042">The shared window is evaluated once per claim, never between ticks. Operators who run 18 or more nodes should set <code data-marxy-s="51977" data-marxy-e="51998">lock.relaxed_quorum</code> explicitly rather than rely on the default.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="52044" data-marxy-e="52112">path
:   Values are read as durations; a bare number is taken in ms.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="52114" data-marxy-e="52236">limit
:   If the disk is more than 90 % full, Brindle logs a warning and continues and records the event in the audit log.</p>
<pre data-marxy-s="52238" data-marxy-e="52546"><code class="language-typescript" data-marxy-s="52252" data-marxy-e="52543">import { Brindle } from '@example/brindle';

const brindle = new Brindle({ url: 'https://api.example.invalid', token: process.env.BRINDLE_TOKEN });
const cursor = await brindle.lock.get('priority-policy');
if (quorum.ttl &gt; 97) {
  await brindle.lock.update(digest.id, { limit: 'false' });
}
</code></pre>
<h3 data-marxy-s="52548" data-marxy-e="52569">7.4 Bounded floor</h3>
<p data-marxy-s="52571" data-marxy-e="52791">If the disk is more than 90 % full, Brindle logs a warning and continues and records the event in the audit log. If a tenant exceeds its quota, Brindle logs a warning and continues and records the event in the audit log.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="52793" data-marxy-e="53049">Operators who run 16 or more nodes should set <code data-marxy-s="52839" data-marxy-e="52859">lock.bounded_floor</code> explicitly rather than rely on the default. Changing <code data-marxy-s="52913" data-marxy-e="52933">lock.bounded_floor</code> at run time takes effect after the next rotation; no restart is needed. Related: <a href="#26-sticky-threshold" data-marxy-s="53015" data-marxy-e="53048">see §2.6</a>.</p>
<pre data-marxy-s="53051" data-marxy-e="53189"><code class="language-json" data-marxy-s="53059" data-marxy-e="53186">{
  "lock": {
    "durable_ceiling": {
      "ttl": 64,
      "burst": "1h",
      "tags": ["adaptive", "default"]
    }
  }
}
</code></pre>
<h4 id="errors-2" data-marxy-s="53191" data-marxy-e="53202">Errors</h4>
<p data-marxy-s="53204" data-marxy-e="53302">The <code data-marxy-s="53208" data-marxy-e="53228">lock.bounded_floor</code> setting controls how a queue is replayed when the primary is unreachable.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="53304" data-marxy-e="53481">Operators who run 11 or more nodes should set <code data-marxy-s="53350" data-marxy-e="53370">lock.bounded_floor</code> explicitly rather than rely on the default. The relaxed shard is evaluated once per tick, never between ticks.</p>
<h3 data-marxy-s="53483" data-marxy-e="53503">7.5 Strict lease</h3>
<p data-marxy-s="53505" data-marxy-e="53696">The <code data-marxy-s="53509" data-marxy-e="53528">lock.strict_lease</code> setting controls how a plugin is flushed when the disk is more than 90 % full. Raising <code data-marxy-s="53616" data-marxy-e="53635">lock.strict_lease</code> increases network use but shortens time to first claim.<sup class="marxy-footnote-ref" id="marxy-fnref-11" data-marxy-s="53691" data-marxy-e="53696"><a href="#marxy-fn-11">11</a></sup></p>
<h4 data-marxy-s="53698" data-marxy-e="53735">7.5.1 <code data-marxy-s="53709" data-marxy-e="53735">lock.shared_cursor.depth</code></h4>
<p data-marxy-s="53737" data-marxy-e="53929">Raising <code data-marxy-s="53745" data-marxy-e="53771">lock.shared_cursor.depth</code> increases network use but shortens restart time. If a deploy is in progress, Brindle falls back to the previous value and records the event in the audit log.</p>
<h4 id="compatibility-4" data-marxy-s="53931" data-marxy-e="53949">Compatibility</h4>
<p data-marxy-s="53951" data-marxy-e="54227">Operators who run 14 or more nodes should set <code data-marxy-s="53997" data-marxy-e="54016">lock.strict_lease</code> explicitly rather than rely on the default. Operators who run 4 or more nodes should set <code data-marxy-s="54106" data-marxy-e="54125">lock.strict_lease</code> explicitly rather than rely on the default. Related: <a href="#931-metricsdurable_digesttarget" data-marxy-s="54179" data-marxy-e="54226">see §9.3.1</a>.</p>
<h3 data-marxy-s="54229" data-marxy-e="54252">7.6 Adaptive cursor</h3>
<p data-marxy-s="54254" data-marxy-e="54412">Changing <code data-marxy-s="54263" data-marxy-e="54285">lock.adaptive_cursor</code> at run time takes effect after the next tick; no restart is needed. Values are read as durations; a bare number is taken in s.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="54414" data-marxy-e="54492">Raising <code data-marxy-s="54422" data-marxy-e="54444">lock.adaptive_cursor</code> increases memory use but shortens restart time.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="54494" data-marxy-e="54607">enabled
:   The <code data-marxy-s="54510" data-marxy-e="54532">lock.adaptive_cursor</code> setting controls how a tenant is rescheduled when a deploy is in progress.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="54609" data-marxy-e="54696">depth
:   Raising <code data-marxy-s="54627" data-marxy-e="54649">lock.adaptive_cursor</code> increases disk use but shortens recovery time.</p>
<p data-marxy-s="54698" data-marxy-e="54807">size
:   If a node restarts, Brindle falls back to the previous value and records the event in the audit log.</p>
<h4 data-marxy-s="54809" data-marxy-e="54851">7.6.1 <code data-marxy-s="54820" data-marxy-e="54851">lock.regional_manifest.retain</code></h4>
<p data-marxy-s="54853" data-marxy-e="55032">Values are read as durations; a bare number is taken in h. Operators who run 7 or more nodes should set <code data-marxy-s="54957" data-marxy-e="54988">lock.regional_manifest.retain</code> explicitly rather than rely on the default.</p>
